[COMP25-20250319-0012]
## Formal Complaint – Diagnostic Anchoring, Bias, and Failure to Reassess (2017–2025)

[COMP25-20250319-0014]
To Whom It May Concern,

[COMP25-20250319-0016]
My name is Liam Moloney.

[COMP25-20250319-0018]
I am writing to raise serious concerns regarding the clinical handling of my case by Galway East Medical Practice (Dr. Aaron Leo Brennan) and the Neurology Department at Galway University Hospitals (Dr. Thomas Monaghan) during the period 2017 to 2025.

[COMP25-20250319-0020]
I submit this complaint with respect for the complexity of clinical decision-making, but also with a sincere request for accountability, transparency, and learning—both in relation to my case and potentially others affected by similar processes.

[COMP25-20250319-0022]
### Background

[COMP25-20250319-0024]
My presenting symptom—sharp, pressure-sensitive foot pain—began in 2005. By 2017, I sought medical intervention through Galway East Medical Practice.

[COMP25-20250319-0026]
From that point, the pain was consistently attributed to peripheral neuropathy, likely alcohol-related, and this framing appears to have followed me into Neurology referrals and specialist reviews.

[COMP25-20250319-0028]
For eight years, despite continued reporting of pain and its impact, the working diagnosis did not materially change, nor was it re-evaluated against emerging symptoms or alternative explanations, including Parkinson's-related pain syndromes or dopamine-responsive conditions.

[COMP25-20250319-0030]
### Concerns Raised

[COMP25-20250319-0032]
I make this complaint fully aware that I do not have access to every internal note, referral, or correspondence generated over these years. However, I am now left with deep concern that the following occurred, or that the system made it impossible to detect if they did:

[COMP25-20250319-0034]
1. **Diagnostic Anchoring and Bias**

[COMP25-20250319-0035]
   - Early framing of my condition as alcohol-related neuropathy became fixed, potentially precluding further investigation.

[COMP25-20250319-0037]
2. **Failure to Reassess**

[COMP25-20250319-0038]
   - Despite deterioration, ongoing pain, and functional impairment, no fresh neurological workup was undertaken that considered Parkinson’s-related pain syndromes or tested dopaminergic responsiveness.

[COMP25-20250319-0040]
3. **Potential Hierarchical Influence**

[COMP25-20250319-0041]
   - I have reason to believe senior clinical influence may have advised caution against certain treatments, which—combined with referral bias—closed off viable therapeutic trials.

[COMP25-20250319-0043]
### The Turning Point

[COMP25-20250319-0045]
In March 2025, I independently trialed Sinemet CR. The result was immediate and sustained relief from two decades of disabling foot pain—effectively disproving the prior working diagnosis and suggesting my condition was dopamine-responsive all along.

[COMP25-20250319-0047]
This raises a fundamental question: Why was this possibility not explored earlier, given the magnitude of harm endured?

[COMP25-20250319-0049]
### The Harm Caused

[COMP25-20250319-0051]
- Physical: Prolonged, preventable pain

[COMP25-20250319-0052]
- Psychological: Fear of movement, cumulative distress, and erosion of resilience

[COMP25-20250319-0053]
- Loss of Trust: A profound impact on my confidence in clinical systems' ability to self-correct

[COMP25-20250319-0055]
### Request for Review and Disclosure

[COMP25-20250319-0057]
I make no presumptions about individual intent or malice. My concern is systemic: that processes of diagnostic framing, confirmation bias, and deference to hierarchy may have overridden patient experience and clinical curiosity.

[COMP25-20250319-0059]
I respectfully request:

[COMP25-20250319-0061]
1. An independent review of my case, with attention to the clinical reasoning applied from 2017 to 2025.

[COMP25-20250319-0062]
2. Full disclosure of my medical records, referrals, specialist correspondence, and any internal memos related to my case during this period.

[COMP25-20250319-0063]
3. A written response explaining how this outcome was possible, and what measures exist to prevent similar diagnostic anchoring from persisting unchecked in other cases.

[COMP25-20250319-0064]
4. Preservation of all records related to my case, including internal communications, pending my full review.

[COMP25-20250319-0066]
### Closing Statement

[COMP25-20250319-0068]
I submit this complaint not in anger, but from a place of humility and genuine need for understanding.

[COMP25-20250319-0070]
I recognise the pressures and complexities of medical practice. However, after eight years of persistent pain and the eventual discovery that relief was possible, I am compelled to ask how the system could have missed this—despite my continued engagement.

[COMP25-20250319-0072]
My aim is not confrontation, but to ensure learning, accountability, and the protection of future patients from similar harm.

[COMP25-20250319-0074]
Yours sincerely,

[COMP25-20250319-0075]
Liam Moloney

[COMP25-20250319-0077]
---

The original DOCX attachment remains the controlling source. The statements above are the patient's contemporaneous assertions and requests; their underlying clinical propositions are not independently established merely because they appear in the complaint.

[REB25-20250325-0049]

[REB25-20250325-0050]

[REB25-20250325-0051]
disease. This includes pain.

[REB25-20250325-0053]

[REB25-20250325-0054]

[REB25-20250325-0055]
disease especially when the effect of the Levodopa Sinemet tablets are wearing off. In particular

understanding of his Parkinson’s disorder. From the very outset the majority of everything that

## QPS250415 — QPS letter

[QPS250415-20250415-0001]
# 15 April 2025 — Quality & Patient Safety complaint letter

[QPS250415-20250415-0003]
**Source type:** primary institutional correspondence

[QPS250415-20250415-0004]
**Author/signatory:** Aoife Nolan, Quality & Patient Safety Coordinator and Complaints Officer, Galway University Hospital

[QPS250415-20250415-0005]
**Recipient:** Liam Moloney

[QPS250415-20250415-0006]
**Date:** 15 April 2025

[QPS250415-20250415-0007]

[QPS250415-20250415-0008]

[QPS250415-20250415-0012]
Galway University Hospitals

[QPS250415-20250415-0013]
Quality & Patient Safety Department GUH

[QPS250415-20250415-0015]
Private & Confidential

[QPS250415-20250415-0017]
Dear Mr Liam Moloney,

[QPS250415-20250415-0019]
I refer to your complaint received by the Quality and Patient Safety Department on the 25th of March in relation to concerns of bias and diagnostic anchoring, that you feel affected how your condition was managed, in particular in relation to pain management.

[QPS250415-20250415-0021]
You recently started Sinemet CR, which has been an effective analgesic for your foot pain. However, you feel this particular medication was not considered by your Neurologist, possibly due to an emphasis on your history of alcohol excess, which led to prolonged and preventable pain.

[QPS250415-20250415-0023]
When a complaint is received by the Complaints Officer and, following its assessment, is determined to relate to matters solely relating to clinical judgment, it must be sent to the appropriate manager for follow-up under the appropriate pathway in accordance with the Health Act 2004. In this regard, your complaint was forwarded for the attention of Consultant Neurologist Dr Thomas Monaghan. I include overleaf a letter from Dr Monaghan in response to the complaint.

[QPS250415-20250415-0025]
In relation to your request for your medical notes, you can request these through Freedom of Information (FOI). I have also enclosed information on how to do this.

[QPS250415-20250415-0027]
Thank you for bringing your concerns to our attention and for the opportunity to respond. We endeavour to improve our service at every opportunity to ensure we provide a high-quality service to those in our care.

[QPS250415-20250415-0029]
With kind regards,

[QPS250415-20250415-0031]
Aoife Nolan

[QPS250415-20250415-0032]
Quality & Patient Safety Coordinator and Complaints Officer

[QPS250415-20250415-0033]
Quality and Patient Safety Department

[QPS250415-20250415-0034]
Galway University Hospital

## ART16250612 — Article 16 clinical request

[ART16250612-20250612-0001]
# 12 June 2025 — GDPR Article 16 Rectification Request

[ART16250612-20250612-0003]
**Source type:** primary contemporaneous patient / data-subject email

[ART16250612-20250612-0004]
**Author:** Liam Moloney

[ART16250612-20250612-0005]
**Recipients:** HSE DPO; DDPO West copied

[ART16250612-20250612-0006]
**Date:** 12 June 2025

[ART16250612-20250612-0007]
**Subject:** `GDPR Rectification Request – Patient Liam Moloney (DOB: 12/11/1977)`

[ART16250612-20250612-0008]
**Provenance:** read directly from the connected Gmail account. This page is a redacted transcription of the message body. No analytical findings are made on this page.

[ART16250612-20250612-0010]
---

[ART16250612-20250612-0012]
Dear Data Protection Officer,

[ART16250612-20250612-0014]
I am writing under Article 16 of the General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR) to formally request rectification and annotation of inaccurate personal data held about me within Galway University Hospitals.

[ART16250612-20250612-0016]
Between 2017 and 2025, I was under the care of your Neurology Department, where I was assigned a diagnosis of idiopathic Parkinson’s disease. However, this diagnosis was reached without incorporating or exploring my primary presenting symptom: bilateral foot pain, which began in 2004–2005 and was the basis for my GP referral in 2017.

[ART16250612-20250612-0018]
In 2025, following my own adjustment of dopaminergic medication (Sinemet CR), I experienced immediate and sustained relief of that symptom — effectively disproving the previously presumed peripheral neuropathy and revealing that the symptom was dopamine-responsive all along.

[ART16250612-20250612-0020]
This finding invalidates critical assumptions underpinning the diagnosis recorded and disseminated across my medical history.

[ART16250612-20250612-0022]
## GDPR Grounds

[ART16250612-20250612-0024]
Under Article 16 of GDPR, I am entitled to request rectification where my data is:

[ART16250612-20250612-0026]
- Inaccurate (e.g., omission of key symptom in referral/consultation notes),

[ART16250612-20250612-0027]
- Misleading (e.g., implication of peripheral neuropathy without confirmatory testing),

[ART16250612-20250612-0028]
- Or incomplete in a clinically significant way (e.g., diagnosis recorded as conclusive despite absence of diagnostic levodopa trial).

[ART16250612-20250612-0030]
The long-standing diagnostic label in my file — and its downstream replication — does not reflect the full, accurate clinical picture, especially now that a dopamine-responsive mechanism has been demonstrated.

[ART16250612-20250612-0032]
## Requested Actions

[ART16250612-20250612-0034]
1. Rectify all relevant documents where the absence of my core symptom (severe, disabling foot pain) misrepresents my diagnostic journey.

[ART16250612-20250612-0035]
2. Annotate all files (referrals, discharge summaries, diagnostic letters) that present a conclusive Parkinson’s diagnosis, noting the lack of dopaminergic diagnostic trial and the subsequent contradictory evidence in 2025.

[ART16250612-20250612-0036]
3. Preserve all internal and external records relating to my case for further review, including MDT notes, email correspondence, and meeting records.

[ART16250612-20250612-0038]
This request is made in good faith, in pursuit of factual accuracy and the correction of records that continue to influence how my health condition is understood, managed, and recorded.

[ART16250612-20250612-0040]
Please confirm receipt of this request and respond within the statutory time frame of one calendar month.

[ART16250612-20250612-0042]
Sincerely,

[ART16250612-20250612-0043]
Liam Moloney

[ART16250612-20250612-0045]
---

[ART16250612-20250612-0047]
**Redaction note:** Home address, date of birth, patient ID and telephone number have been omitted from this repository transcription. The original Gmail message remains the controlling source. Statements about diagnosis, causation and disproving prior assumptions are the data subject's assertions in the request, not findings made by this source page.

## QPS250613 — QPS IMF letter

[QPS250613-20250613-0001]
# 13 June 2025 — QPS letter concerning complaint handling and IMF review

[QPS250613-20250613-0003]
**Source type:** primary institutional correspondence

[QPS250613-20250613-0004]
**Author/signatory:** Aoife Nolan, Quality & Patient Safety Coordinator and Complaints Officer, Galway University Hospital

[QPS250613-20250613-0005]
**Recipient:** Liam Moloney

[QPS250613-20250613-0006]
**Date:** 13 June 2025

[QPS250613-20250613-0007]
**Provenance:** transcribed from two photographed pages preserved in Gmail and directly visually inspected.

[QPS250613-20250613-0008]
**Status:** faithful redacted transcription of the substantive correspondence. The original photographs remain controlling. No analytical findings are made on this page.

[QPS250613-20250613-0010]
---

[QPS250613-20250613-0012]
Dear Mr. Moloney,

[QPS250613-20250613-0014]
I have received communication arising from the Office of the Ombudsman in relation to apparent concerns which you have raised about the management of your complaint, which was first received by the Quality and Patient Safety Department on the 25th of March 2025.

[QPS250613-20250613-0016]
I understand you have expressed concern with regard to the objectivity of the hospital’s approach to the management of your complaint. The purpose of my correspondence to you today is to provide you with additional clarity with regard to how complaints are managed.

[QPS250613-20250613-0018]
The HSE Your Service Your Say (YSYS) Policy 2017 is underpinned by Part 9 of the 2004 Health Act and 2006 Complaints Regulations. This legislation exempts certain matters for examination under the YSYS policy. Section 48(1)(a) outlines that a person is not entitled to make a complaint about a matter relating solely to the exercise of clinical judgement by a person acting on behalf of the Executive (HSE).

[QPS250613-20250613-0020]
As outlined in the letter of response issued on the 15th of April, a complaint that relates to matters of clinical judgment is always made available to the clinician whom the complaint is about, for their observations and response where appropriate. This is to afford the clinician an opportunity to respond to what has been alleged. Please note therefore that the role of a Complaints Officer in the case of matters of clinical judgment is not to investigate the matter, but to simply coordinate the management of the complaint. This involves ensuring that the complaint is received by the correct clinician, and that a response is provided back to the complainant. It is important to emphasise here that as matters of clinical judgement are exempt from examination under the YSYS policy, they are not subject to a Stage 3 and Stage 4 Review.

[QPS250613-20250613-0022]
As you were not reassured by the response provided to you on 25th April, I consulted with the Clinical Director for Medicine in GUH, who agreed to have your complaint reviewed independently by the hospital’s incident management team to allay your concerns about transparency. You were advised of this in a letter issued to you on the 30th of May.

[QPS250613-20250613-0024]
### YSYS

[QPS250613-20250613-0026]
My role is to ensure a complaint that relates to a clinical issue is referred to the respective clinician who provided care, and to issue this response to the complainant in due course.

[QPS250613-20250613-0028]
### Incident Management Framework (IMF)

[QPS250613-20250613-0030]
Your complaint will be reviewed independently by an incident management team in accordance with this framework.

[QPS250613-20250613-0032]
### Ongoing correspondence

[QPS250613-20250613-0034]
I wish to thank you for your correspondence to date and the information which you have provided. Given the volume of correspondence received to date in relation to your complaint, and my commitment to a high volume of current open cases within my remit, I will require some time to review, collate and coordinate your complaint. In that regard, I ask that you kindly refrain from forwarding me any further correspondence and documentation at this time as this will only deter from advancing and progressing your complaint. I undertake to revert to you once the relevant personnel have reviewed your case and a response has been prepared.

[QPS250613-20250613-0036]
I hope my correspondence has provided you with more clarity on the process and assurance that I play no part in the examination and further review of your complaint.

[QPS250613-20250613-0038]
Yours sincerely,

[QPS250613-20250613-0040]
Aoife Nolan

[QPS250613-20250613-0041]
Quality & Patient Safety Coordinator and Complaints Officer

[QPS250613-20250613-0042]
Quality and Patient Safety Department

[QPS250613-20250613-0043]
Galway University Hospital

[QPS250613-20250613-0045]
---

[QPS250613-20250613-0047]
**Source-boundary note:** This source directly establishes the hospital’s stated process position on 13 June 2025, including the distinction it drew between YSYS handling of clinical-judgment complaints and the separate IMF route, and its representation that the incident-management review would be independent. It does not establish what evidence the later PAR/SIMT actually used or whether proposition-level independent revalidation occurred.

[ART15250707-20250707-0011]
The data subject requested access under Article 15 GDPR to personal data and institutional documentation concerning a bedside teaching interaction during the July 2017 admission.

[ART15250707-20250707-0013]
The request recorded the data subject's account that four medical trainees were present; that he raised concern that disabling bilateral foot pain had not been addressed; that he asked for the supervising neurologist; and that a discussion followed concerning podiatry.

[ART15250707-20250707-0015]
The request sought:

[ART15250707-20250707-0017]
- clinical documentation from 10–11 July 2017 referring to the interaction or student involvement;

[ART15250707-20250707-0018]
- student logbooks, observation records or teaching notes;

[ART15250707-20250707-0019]
- ward-round records, consultant entries or trainee annotations referring to symptoms, foot pain or the teaching interaction;

[ART15250707-20250707-0020]
- governance, supervisory or educational material describing bedside teaching on the ward during the period; and

[ART15250707-20250707-0021]
- trainee assignment records or rosters identifying students present.

[ART15250707-20250707-0023]
The request asked that third-party information be redacted rather than used as a basis for wholesale refusal.

[ART15250707-20250707-0025]
---

[ART15250707-20250707-0027]
**Source-boundary note:** This source directly establishes the content and scope of the 7 July 2025 access request. The described bedside interaction remains a patient account unless independently corroborated.

[ART16250716-20250716-0011]
The email described itself as a final and comprehensive Article 16 request following the 12 June and 15 June requests and the 7 July reminders. It alleged non-compliance with Article 12(3), invited corrective action, and attached a document titled **“Final Article 16 GDPR Rectification Request — Rectification of Structurally Contradictory Medical Record.”**

[ART16250716-20250716-0013]
The attachment advanced the data subject's then-current case that the medical record was structurally inaccurate or incomplete. Among other things, it relied on the absence of a diagnostic levodopa trial, the bilateral foot-pain history, the 2025 dopamine-response discovery, and concerns about how specialist expertise and later complaint handling were represented. It sought rectification/annotation of diagnostic labels and summaries, restrictions on secondary use, and disclosure of governance decisions and teaching use.

[ART16250716-20250716-0015]
The document also contained strong legal and clinical assertions, including that the record was “structurally and clinically self-nullifying,” that the 2025 response “disproved” earlier assumptions, and that the diagnosis was unsound.

[ART16250716-20250716-0017]

## MCCOUN25 — Medical Council Counihan decision

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0001]
# 29 July 2025 - Medical Council CEO decision regarding Dr Timothy Counihan

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0003]
**Source type:** primary institutional/regulatory decision; derivative as to underlying historical clinical facts

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0004]
**Author:** CEO, Medical Council of Ireland

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0005]
**Recipient:** Liam Moloney

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0006]
**Reference:** R016/25

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0007]
**Date:** 29 July 2025

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0008]
**Provenance:** transcribed from `Eletter with encl. to Mr Liam Moloney re. Dr Timothy Counihan dated 29.07.25.pdf`, located in the project/Gmail evidence and directly inspected.

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0009]
**Status:** faithful redacted transcription. The original PDF remains the controlling source. No analytical findings are made on this page.

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0011]
---

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0013]
Dear Mr Moloney,

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0015]
I am writing to you in respect of the complaint you made in relation to Dr Timothy Counihan received on 9 May 2025. Under the Medical Practitioners Act 2007, as amended (“the Act”), complaints are made to, and investigated by, the Chief Executive Officer (“CEO”) of the Medical Council.

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0017]
Please note that the CEO has made a decision not to investigate your complaint against Dr Counihan. The CEO’s decision and the basis for it are explained further below.

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0019]
## The Decision of the CEO

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0021]
Before a decision is made to investigate a complaint, the CEO is required to consider whether he or she:

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0023]
i. is satisfied that the complaint is not made in good faith, or

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0024]
ii. is satisfied that the complaint is frivolous or vexatious.

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0026]
If the CEO decides that a complaint comes within one of the above categories, the complaint will be closed. If the complaint does not come within one of these categories, then the CEO is required to investigate the complaint.

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0028]
The CEO has considered this complaint and decided that the complaint was frivolous and/or vexatious in circumstances where Dr Counihan was **not directly involved in your care** and was satisfied that the complaint did not raise any clinical concerns or patient safety issues in relation to Dr Counihan and could never reach the threshold of seriousness to warrant a referral to the Fitness to Practise Committee.

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0030]
## What Happens Next

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0032]
Please note that the CEO has made a decision not to investigate your complaint against Dr Counihan. On that basis, no further action will be taken in relation to your complaint against Dr Counihan.

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0034]
An Authorised Officer will be in touch with you in due course in relation to your complaint against Dr Thomas Monaghan.

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0036]
Please note that a copy of your complaint together with the CEO’s decision outlined above has also been sent to Dr Counihan for his information.

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0038]
## General Information regarding the Medical Council Complaint Process

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0040]
The Medical Council is the regulatory body for doctors. It has a statutory role in protecting the public by promoting the highest professional standards amongst doctors practising in the Republic of Ireland. One of the functions of the Medical Council is to consider complaints against registered medical practitioners. The CEO of the Medical Council considers and investigates complaints against registered medical practitioners. Part 7 of the Act is the part that relates to the consideration of complaints by the CEO. Section 57 (2B) is the section of the Act that deals with the triaging of complaints by the CEO.

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0042]
Should you have any further queries please do not hesitate to contact complaints@mcirl.ie.

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0044]
Yours sincerely,

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0046]
CEO

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0047]
Sent electronically and accordingly bears no signature

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0049]
---

[MCCOUN25-20250729-0051]
**Redaction / scope note:** Postal address, general regulator contact details and administrative formatting are omitted. The phrase **“not directly involved in your care”** is retained exactly because its wording is evidentially material. The decision establishes the regulator's 2025 decision and stated basis; it is not, without its source architecture, independent proof of the exact scope of any 2017 collegiate advice.

## MCMON25 — Monaghan Medical Council response

[MCMON25-20250729-0001]
# 29 July 2025 - Dr Thomas Monaghan submission to the Medical Council

[MCMON25-20250729-0003]
**Source type:** primary later clinician/regulatory submission

[MCMON25-20250729-0004]
**Author:** Dr Thomas Monaghan, Consultant Neurologist

[MCMON25-20250729-0005]
**Recipient:** Ms Gemma Fallon, Authorised Officer of the CEO, Medical Council of Ireland

[MCMON25-20250729-0006]
**Reference:** R / 016 / 25

[MCMON25-20250729-0007]
**Date:** 29 July 2025

[MCMON25-20250729-0008]
**Provenance:** transcribed from `Response from Dr Thomas Monaghan dated 29.07.25.pdf`, located in the connected Gmail/project evidence and directly extracted from the five-page PDF.

[MCMON25-20250729-0009]
**Status:** faithful redacted transcription. Page furniture, addresses and repeated footer text are omitted; wording of the substantive body is retained, including source grammar/typographical irregularities. The original PDF remains the controlling source. No analytical findings are made on this page.

[MCMON25-20250729-0011]
---

[MCMON25-20250729-0013]
Dear Ms Fallon,

[MCMON25-20250729-0015]
Thank you for your letter.

[MCMON25-20250729-0017]
I am of course personally devastated to be in receipt of this.

[MCMON25-20250729-0019]
However that is not what matters: It is the experience of the person that is at the heart of this that matters, and it is the assessment and the judgement of the Medical Council in your role to protect patients, that matters.

[MCMON25-20250729-0021]
Mr Moloney made a complaint to University Hospital Galway in March 2025. I answered this immediately to the best of my ability to the Hospital, and in the knowledge that that reply would be copied to Mr Moloney. Mr Moloney had specified he did not want to have contact with clinicians until he was in receipt of what he requested.

[MCMON25-20250729-0023]
The Hospital supplied me with a subsequent complaint on the 24th of April 2025. Due to technical issues, I was delayed in sending the reply to the Hospital in respect of this until the evening of the 25th of April 2025.

[MCMON25-20250729-0025]
On the 28th of April 2025, while the second complaint was in process with University Hospital Galway, I was informed by the Hospital of a third complaint - a re-framing as he explained of the first complaint.

[MCMON25-20250729-0027]
In answering each of the first two complaints: I have at length explained my thinking and my medical management of Mr Moloney's medical needs as per attached.

[MCMON25-20250729-0029]
I believe these to have been correct and appropriate.

[MCMON25-20250729-0031]
I understand and respect that Mr Moloney does not agree.

[MCMON25-20250729-0033]
If I may summarise what I believe to be my understanding of his position in the complaints received in the Hospital: this is that I have framed everything in the context of an unproven neuropathy, being the cause of his symptoms, and I have “blamed alcohol”.

[MCMON25-20250729-0035]
I think there is prima facie evidence as per the documentation enclosed, that this is not correct.

[MCMON25-20250729-0037]
I think the detailed explanations explored in my responses to his complaints make this clear.

[MCMON25-20250729-0039]
In point of fact Mr Moloney has been on treatment with Levodopa (Sinemet) since 2021 and in another forms, and we have known and documented that his has had a beneficial response for him in terms of pain. We had also tried to used synthetic Dopamine agents since 2018 (discontinued due to adverse effect/intolerances related to dopaminergic treatment)

[MCMON25-20250729-0041]
His contention would seem to be that “Sinemet CR” (Controlled release Levodopa) started earlier would have given him sooner relief of pain and other leg based symptoms).

[MCMON25-20250729-0043]
To be of assistance to the Medical Council I have enclosed all of the documentation to answer Mr Moloney's complaints, that I have supplied to the University Hospital Galway.

[MCMON25-20250729-0045]
In Particular there is:

[MCMON25-20250729-0047]
- SECTION 1 - The reply to Mr Moloney's first complaint. This explains in detail my clinical reasoning.

[MCMON25-20250729-0048]
- This also includes (APPENDIX 1) the working document of his clinical records that I have going back to 2017.

[MCMON25-20250729-0049]
- I asked the Hospital to provide my response to his complaint letter, unedited and un-redacted to Mr Moloney (as opposed to a draft of a Hospital Response letter formulated incorporating parts of my answers). I asked the Hospital to provide all of my working file - he requested his data, and he is of course completely entitled to this. (APPENDIX 1). This was to save Mr Moloney having to go through a separate FoI process, so he could have much of the data that he requested, and at speed.

[MCMON25-20250729-0050]
- In APPENDIX 2 - I have enclosed Mr Moloney's original complaint to the Hospital.

[MCMON25-20250729-0051]
- In APPENDIX 3 - I have the reply that was formulated to his additional complaint and his additional questions, when Mr Moloney expressed he was dissatisfied with the Hospital's response and closure of his case (sent by me to the Hospital on the 25th of April 2025).

[MCMON25-20250729-0052]
- In APPENDIX 4 - I have the reply formulated to Mr Moloney's re-framing third complaint to the Hospital. It was at this time I became aware of Mr Moloney's letter to a Neurology colleague Prof Timothy J Counihan, of his concerns as regards Prof Counihan’s advice to myself (in 2017 when we had reviewed Mr Moloney's care at our Neurology Dept Weekly Complex Case meeting). Prof Counihan had no further involvement formally or otherwise in the management of Mr Moloney’s care. Prof Counihan’s advice to myself of that time was only what I already knew to be the case: the necessity for care in the commencement/escalation of Levodopa treatment in a younger person with Parkinsons Disease.

[MCMON25-20250729-0053]
- It was at this time (28th April 2025) that I became aware of the complaint that had been made to the Medical Council, and accordingly a draft of this current document was commenced as of the 28th of April 2025, in anticipation of your now current request of 29th July 2025.

[MCMON25-20250729-0054]
- APPENDIX 5 - Email to the Directorate of Medicine UHG

[MCMON25-20250729-0055]
- APPENDIX 6 - GDPR request for Mr Moloney to have his Medical Records Changed or Annotated.

[MCMON25-20250729-0056]
- (Appendix 7 - the Medical Council complaint - not enclosed, this document is reply)

[MCMON25-20250729-0058]
I accept I may be wrong - but I think and hope that my management was correct.

[MCMON25-20250729-0060]
From 2017, from Mr Moloney's very first inpatient evaluation - I had noted and explored with him that we would try to delay treatment with Levodopa for as long as possible, and then we would also try to delay escalation.

[MCMON25-20250729-0062]
Indeed it was also discussed back then that he may not be happy with this delay.

[MCMON25-20250729-0064]
This was in an effort to protect his overall health and wellbeing in the most holistic of manners. This was only done sincerely, and with his best interests at heart.

[MCMON25-20250729-0066]
Specifically this was to avoid the risk of Dopamine treatment related complications, such as Dyskinesias (abnormal movements); Dopaminergic treatment related Impulse Control disorders / Dopa Dysregulation Syndrome.

[MCMON25-20250729-0068]
Quite simply there is no other conceivable reason that a doctor could or would wish to delay treatment with an agent for symptomatic relief, other than to try to protect the person from adverse outcome.

[MCMON25-20250729-0070]
This was my goal: to try to protect him from the effects of a treatment that we all knew and understood that Mr Moloney would come to need more and more of over time.

[MCMON25-20250729-0072]
As the extensive documentation provided will show evidence of, at every step of the process in his annual reviews, everything that I explored and explained was as documented.

[MCMON25-20250729-0074]
In each instance Mr Moloney was kept aware of everything by this being explained, and his being copied on his own letters.

[MCMON25-20250729-0076]
In the rest of this particular document, to you as the Medical Council, I have not sought to answer the specifics of Mr Moloney’s complaints as we understood them already.

[MCMON25-20250729-0078]
This is simply because I have explored this complex matter which has extended over the 8 years since I have known Mr Moloney, in the detailed answers to his complaints as attached in the associated document.

[MCMON25-20250729-0080]
I have left this in because there has been an evolution of the complaints that Mr Moloney has made.

[MCMON25-20250729-0082]
I am ethically bound and I understand legally bound in terms of "open disclosure" to lay out all the facts/interpretations as I know them to be.

[MCMON25-20250729-0084]
Because Mr Moloney’s complaints to the Medical Council may further evolve, beyond the documentation you provided me with dated the 25th of April, I have therefore enclosed all of this documentation (clinical and complaint management), in the interests of full disclosure.

[MCMON25-20250729-0086]
However to more specifically address aspects of the items from the complaint dated 25th of April 2025, that the Medical Council has supplied me with today 2025-07-29th

[MCMON25-20250729-0088]
## Mr Moloney’s Complaint “II The Core Admission”

[MCMON25-20250729-0090]
I am a general neurologist in public practice only. I have worked in UHG since 2012 (13 years as a neurologist). I have worked only in Neurology since July 2002 (i.e. for 23 years)

[MCMON25-20250729-0092]
It is the normal expectation of any general neurologist. that they would be used to managing patients with a wide range of neurological conditions.

[MCMON25-20250729-0094]
If I may give another example, in our own department we do not have a specific "multiple sclerosis" neurologist, but all of my colleagues manage patients with multiple sclerosis, young and old.

[MCMON25-20250729-0096]
While there is one Neurologist in our department with a background in movement disorders, as you can understand: it would be impossible for one person to be able to manage everybody in our region with Parkinson's and other movement disorders.

[MCMON25-20250729-0098]
(To my regret, it is now my understanding that Mr Moloney has made a Medical Council complaint against that Neurologist as well, simply on the basis of an original discussion of the need for care with Levodopa Sinemet therapy).

[MCMON25-20250729-0100]
Furthermore in that complaint Mr Moloney states that no referral was made to a movement disorder specialist.

[MCMON25-20250729-0102]
This is not correct.

[MCMON25-20250729-0104]
Mr Moloney was referred on the basis of his very young age to Professor Dan Healy, Consultant Neurologist at Beaumont Hospital, for a second opinion.

[MCMON25-20250729-0106]
## Mr Moloney’s Complaint “III Professional and legal obligations triggered.”

[MCMON25-20250729-0108]
I can only say that I felt that Mr Moloney's management with myself was within the limits of my competence.

[MCMON25-20250729-0110]
## Mr Moloney’s Complaint “IV What was not done.”

[MCMON25-20250729-0112]
As explained just above Mr Moloney was seen by Professor Dan Healy.

[MCMON25-20250729-0114]
As regards the description of Mr Moloney’s “Section IV 2. No Trial of First Line Therapy”

[MCMON25-20250729-0116]
It was carefully explained and explored with Mr Moloney at the very beginning why we would try to defer and limit the initiation of Sinemet.

[MCMON25-20250729-0118]
As noted above, this was to try to protect Mr Moloney from the risks of Levodopa adverse effects such as impulse control disorders, dopamine dysregulation syndrome, dyskinesia (an abnormal movement disorder).

[MCMON25-20250729-0120]
It was explained from the outset that sooner or later this type of treatment (Sinemet Levodopa/carbidopa) would be necessary and commenced and indeed it was explained at the very beginning that Mr Moloney may then wish that we had done this earlier.

[MCMON25-20250729-0122]
The Levodopa commencement was in 2021 (Sinemet in the ordinary form, not controlled release). Other dopamine “based” treatment had been tried already since November 2018.

[MCMON25-20250729-0124]
I feel that all of the other aspects of section 4 are answered elsewhere.

[MCMON25-20250729-0126]
## Mr Moloney’s Complaint “V What harm followed.”

[MCMON25-20250729-0128]
I think that all of the aspects of this section are answered in the associated documentation.

[MCMON25-20250729-0130]
If I may observe, it was ourselves that had prescribed the Sinemet CR in March 2025.

[MCMON25-20250729-0132]
May I also note once again that in reality, Sinemet had been started approximately 4 years earlier already in 2021.

[MCMON25-20250729-0134]
In this section 5 Mr Moloney talks of "the working diagnosis of 'alcohol related peripheral neuropathy'".

[MCMON25-20250729-0136]
I think it is clear from the associated documentation as per the evidence enclosed, that we had discussed the risk of a peripheral neuropathy and the worry that Mr Moloney may have a peripheral neuropathy.

[MCMON25-20250729-0138]
I think it will be clear to the Medical Council that there was not the diagnosis of a peripheral neuropathy to the exclusion of other considerations nor his Parkinsons disease.

[MCMON25-20250729-0140]
This was even to the extent that tests were done for example for the overlap disorder of Fabry's disease wherein a person may have peripheral neuropathic features and Parkinsonism.

[MCMON25-20250729-0142]
## Mr Moloney’s Complaint “VI Why this demands Scrutiny.”

[MCMON25-20250729-0144]
In section B where Mr Moloney states "this was not caution - it was negligence".

[MCMON25-20250729-0146]
"A subspecialist was never brought in"

[MCMON25-20250729-0148]
- as detailed above this is not true.

[MCMON25-20250729-0150]
"A first line treatment Levodopa was never offered".

[MCMON25-20250729-0152]
- This is not true. Levodopa Sinemet ordinary form had been used since 2021, and Dopamine based treatment had been tried in Nevember 2018.

[MCMON25-20250729-0154]
"Unproven diagnosis was never revisited".

[MCMON25-20250729-0156]
- It was not definitively stated that Mr Moloney had a peripheral neuropathy. But it was I think it will be agreed a reasonable concern. I think that the enclosed documentation will prove to the satisfaction of the Medical Council and it is Expert Designates that we had Mr Moloney's diagnosis of Parkinson's front and central in every clinical consideration.

[MCMON25-20250729-0158]
I have always myself seen Mr Moloney usually mainly on my own at his clinic visits. I have arranged his appointments in more recent years separate to the usual clinic so that he might have more time.

[MCMON25-20250729-0160]
As per my response to his complaint it would always have been my view that we got on very well and that we were on the best of terms, with a really very good relationship.

[MCMON25-20250729-0162]
I deeply regret this outcome on a personal and a professional level.

[MCMON25-20250729-0164]
I will provide you with whatever else that the Medical Council request / require, as I am obliged to do, to assist you in any way possible with this matter.

[MCMON25-20250729-0166]
I will of course be subject to whatever your determination is, and whatever your judgement will be in this matter.

[MCMON25-20250729-0168]
I have used the Hospital email for this letter as opposed to my usual commercial email provider address (as is registered with the Medial Council), simply for data protection reasons.

[MCMON25-20250729-0170]
I do hope this is of assistance.

[MCMON25-20250729-0172]
If you have any questions or concerns please do not hesitate to contact me.

[MCMON25-20250729-0174]
Thank you,

[MCMON25-20250729-0176]
Yours sincerely,

[MCMON25-20250729-0178]
Dr Thomas Monaghan MB BCh BAO (NUI 1999), MRCPI

[MCMON25-20250729-0179]
Consultant Neurologist

[MCMON25-20250729-0181]
---

[MCMON25-20250729-0183]
**Redaction / transcription note:** Institutional and home addresses, registration number, page numbers and repeated document footer are omitted. The source's substantive wording is retained. This is a retrospective 2025 clinician submission: it directly proves what Dr Monaghan represented to the Medical Council in 2025, including his description of Prof Counihan's advice and the Weekly Complex Case meeting; it does not replace the native 2017 correspondence or meeting record for proving the exact historical interaction.

## MONSUP25 — Monaghan supporting-documentation index

[MONSUP25-20250729-0001]
# 29 July 2025 — Dr Thomas Monaghan supporting documentation bundle

[MONSUP25-20250729-0003]
**Source type:** primary later clinician/regulatory source bundle containing reproduced earlier clinical records and complaint material

[MONSUP25-20250729-0004]
**Supplying clinician:** Dr Thomas Monaghan, Consultant Neurologist

[MONSUP25-20250729-0005]
**Date supplied:** 29 July 2025

[MONSUP25-20250729-0006]
**File inspected:** `Redacted Supporting documentation rec'd from Dr Thomas Monghan dated 29.07.25.pdf`

[MONSUP25-20250729-0007]
**Length:** 185 pages

[MONSUP25-20250729-0008]
**Provenance:** the complete PDF was supplied directly to the project and inspected in rendered form and parsed text.

[MONSUP25-20250729-0009]
**Status:** source-bundle index only. The original PDF remains the controlling source. This page does not convert reproduced historical material into a new independent evidential origin and makes no medico-legal finding.

[MONSUP25-20250729-0011]
---

[MONSUP25-20250729-0013]
## Documentary structure

[MONSUP25-20250729-0015]
The bundle begins with Dr Monaghan's 25 March 2025 response to the hospital complaint and then identifies:

[MONSUP25-20250729-0017]
- **Appendix 1:** his current working clinical document file containing older information up to approximately 10 March 2025;

[MONSUP25-20250729-0018]
- **Appendix 2:** Mr Moloney's complaint/account;

[MONSUP25-20250729-0019]
- later complaint-response and governance material supplied in support of the clinician's July 2025 regulatory response.

[MONSUP25-20250729-0021]
Dr Monaghan states in the bundle that his working file was being supplied so that the documentary basis of his clinical reasoning could be reviewed and that the material included records going back to 2017.

[MONSUP25-20250729-0023]
---

[MONSUP25-20250729-0025]
## Reproduced 2017 clinical record

[MONSUP25-20250729-0027]
The working clinical file reproduces the 2017 presentation and examination, including:

[MONSUP25-20250729-0029]
- longstanding lower-limb tension and later corns/calluses and foot pain;

[MONSUP25-20250729-0030]
- markedly increased tone, gross bradykinesia and a strikingly abnormal gait;

[MONSUP25-20250729-0031]
- a footdrop-like appearance described as somewhat suggestive of a dystonic posture and expressly **not foot drop**;

[MONSUP25-20250729-0032]
- pes planus and hammertoes;

[MONSUP25-20250729-0033]
- severe young-onset parkinsonism with an open atypical/genetic differential;

[MONSUP25-20250729-0034]
- later abnormal DaTSCAN evidence of bilateral dopaminergic deficit.

[MONSUP25-20250729-0036]
The same reproduced 2017 record states that the leg symptoms, corns/calluses and burning **“may have been completely separate”** or possibly related to a peripheral neuropathy and records the conclusion **“Not clear.”**

[MONSUP25-20250729-0038]
The plan then separately requests outpatient Chiropody/Podiatry services and records that an SHO had tried to arrange this in hospital.

[MONSUP25-20250729-0040]
### Source boundary

[MONSUP25-20250729-0042]
This bundle is a 2025 reproduction of the clinician's working record. It is primary evidence of what Dr Monaghan supplied in 2025 and strong documentary evidence of the content of his retained working file. Where the repository already holds contemporaneous 2017 source pages, those earlier records remain the preferable source for proving the 2017 event itself.

[MONSUP25-20250729-0044]
---

[MONSUP25-20250729-0046]
## 2017 treatment reasoning and genetics

[MONSUP25-20250729-0048]
The reproduced 2017 material records a plan to proceed cautiously with conventional dopaminergic therapy because of Liam's very young age and concern about dyskinesia and impulse-control complications. It also records that the case had been considered at a Neurology Complex Case meeting and that care with later levodopa/dopamine-agonist treatment had been advised with Prof Counihan and Neurology colleagues.

[MONSUP25-20250729-0050]
The same 2017 working record expressly raises a possible **PRKN / PARK2** explanation because of the very young onset, symmetry, akinetic-rigid presentation, lack of a dominant family history and preserved cognition. It records that PRKN/PARK2 testing or a broader panel was a reasonable option and refers to Prof Healy's interest in that possibility.

[MONSUP25-20250729-0052]
### Source boundary

[MONSUP25-20250729-0054]
The 2017 material establishes that PRKN/PARK2 was a contemporaneous diagnostic possibility, not that a PRKN diagnosis had been made. The later 2026 specialist genetic result is a separate evidential origin establishing the eventual genetic diagnosis.

[MONSUP25-20250729-0056]
---

[MONSUP25-20250729-0058]
## April 2018 Procyclidine withdrawal history

[MONSUP25-20250729-0060]
The working file reproduces the 24 April 2018 Neurology record. It states that the Parkinson nurse had suggested stopping Procyclidine for approximately two weeks in Sept/Oct 2017 because of blurred vision and that, during that stop:

[MONSUP25-20250729-0062]
> “the first thing he noticed changing and changing rapidly was the return on the foot, of the outside left corn.”

[MONSUP25-20250729-0064]
The same passage records that symptoms worsened somewhat and that Liam was more **“shifty”** and **“unrelaxed”** off Procyclidine.

[MONSUP25-20250729-0066]
This is the same early treatment-response episode separately represented in the repository's 2018 Neurology source page and contemporaneous 24 October 2017 patient email.

[MONSUP25-20250729-0068]
---

[MONSUP25-20250729-0070]
## 2020 lower-limb / foot findings

[MONSUP25-20250729-0072]
The working file reproduces the 2020 Neurology material describing:

[MONSUP25-20250729-0074]
- a bad corn on the outside of the left foot;

[MONSUP25-20250729-0075]
- a left lower-limb “throw” with preserved dorsiflexion, plantarflexion, inversion and eversion power;

[MONSUP25-20250729-0076]
- the clinician's conclusion that this foot-throw appearance was parkinsonian rather than true foot drop;

[MONSUP25-20250729-0077]
- exquisite sensitivity of the feet;

[MONSUP25-20250729-0078]
- excessive movement and urge to move the feet.

[MONSUP25-20250729-0080]
These are later direct clinical observations and should not be treated as retrospective proof that every component of the 2017 foot syndrome was neurological.

[MONSUP25-20250729-0082]
---

[MONSUP25-20250729-0084]
## October 2021 Sinemet and DBS material

[MONSUP25-20250729-0086]
The working file reproduces the October 2021 clinical record stating that ordinary Sinemet had first been introduced in September 2021 and increased as Kemadrin was withdrawn because of ocular-pressure/visual concerns.

[MONSUP25-20250729-0088]
The same record says Liam had been fully counselled and would receive a copy at home, followed by the categorical statement:

[MONSUP25-20250729-0090]
> “the DBS is only ever for the tremor element. It will not help with anything else.”

[MONSUP25-20250729-0092]
A narrower patient-specific clinical judgment may have been intended, but this bundle preserves the categorical wording actually recorded and supplied to the patient.

[MONSUP25-20250729-0094]
---

[MONSUP25-20250729-0096]
## March 2025 complaint-response material

[MONSUP25-20250729-0098]
The opening complaint response states that Sinemet had been known to reduce Liam's experience of pain and that Dr Monaghan's own 10 March 2025 Sinemet CR prescription was **“also to try to help the morning experience including of pain.”**

[MONSUP25-20250729-0100]
It further records that the recent Sinemet CR escalation had provided **“profound relief currently.”**

[MONSUP25-20250729-0102]
The clinician's explanation remains that pain could have mixed or peripheral causes and that Parkinsonism/dopaminergic state could magnify the experience of pain; the response also preserves the stated rationale for cautious dose escalation in a young patient because of dyskinesia and dopamine-dysregulation/impulse-control risks.

[MONSUP25-20250729-0104]
---

[MONSUP25-20250729-0106]
## Information supplied to the patient

[MONSUP25-20250729-0108]
Across the working file Dr Monaghan repeatedly states that his long clinic letters were intended to be copied to Liam and to create a shared understanding of what had been discussed, why decisions had been made and when.

[MONSUP25-20250729-0110]
This is evidence about the information framework the treating clinician says he deliberately supplied. It does not prove that Liam agreed with every proposition or that every discussion was fully understood in the same way by clinician and patient.

[MONSUP25-20250729-0112]
---

[MONSUP25-20250729-0114]
## Evidential use

[MONSUP25-20250729-0116]
This bundle is especially useful for **source genealogy** because it shows which historical clinical records and propositions Dr Monaghan himself selected and supplied in 2025 when explaining and defending the earlier management.

[MONSUP25-20250729-0118]
It must not be counted as independent corroboration of historical propositions merely because those propositions reappear inside a later regulatory bundle. The evidential origin remains the underlying historical record unless the 2025 document adds a genuinely new later statement, explanation or admission.

[MONSUP25-20250729-0120]
Key separate later propositions originating in the 2025 response include:

[MONSUP25-20250729-0122]
- Dr Monaghan's retrospective explanation of his reasoning;

[MONSUP25-20250729-0123]
- his statement that the 10 March 2025 Sinemet CR prescription was intended to help the morning experience including pain;

[MONSUP25-20250729-0124]
- his description of the current response as profound relief;

[MONSUP25-20250729-0125]
- his description of how and why his letters were intended to inform Liam;

[MONSUP25-20250729-0126]
- his retrospective account of Prof Counihan/Complex Case involvement, where not independently established by contemporaneous meeting material.

[MONSUP25-20250729-0128]
---

[ART18250804-20250804-0012]
I note the initiation of a serious‑incident review (SIMT). My Article 16 rectification request remains outstanding; I also invoke Article 18 to restrict processing of the disputed diagnosis and related entries pending correction/annotation. Please (i) confirm a data‑clean protocol so the review does not rely on inaccurate data; (ii) provide the Terms of Reference, data sources/versions, and audit trail for any extracts; and (iii) acknowledge a litigation hold over all relevant records. This correspondence is without prejudice to judicial review, negligence, and GDPR civil proceedings.

[ART18250804-20250804-0014]
---

[ART18250804-20250804-0016]
**Source-status note:** This email proves that Article 18 restriction was expressly invoked and that both DPO addresses and QPS were addressees. It does not by itself establish whether restriction was accepted, implemented, refused, qualified, lifted, or whether subsequent processing fell within any lawful exception. The original Gmail message remains the controlling source.

## SIMT250901 — Signed SIMT outcome

[SIMT250901-20250901-0001]
# 1 September 2025 - Signed SIMT outcome letter

[SIMT250901-20250901-0003]
**Source type:** primary institutional/governance decision artefact; derivative as to underlying historical clinical facts

[SIMT250901-20250901-0004]
**NIMS reference:** 25634344

[SIMT250901-20250901-0005]
**Authors/signatories:** Ms Aoife Nolan, Quality and Patient Safety Coordinator, Galway University Hospital; Professor Tim O'Brien, Clinical Director, Medicine MCAN, HSE West and North West

[SIMT250901-20250901-0006]
**Recipient:** Liam Moloney

[SIMT250901-20250901-0007]
**Date on letter:** 1 September 2025

[SIMT250901-20250901-0008]
**SIMT meeting date stated:** 27 August 2025

[SIMT250901-20250901-0009]
**Provenance:** photographed signed letter (`1000068666.jpg`) located as a Gmail attachment and directly visually inspected.

[SIMT250901-20250901-0010]
**Status:** faithful redacted transcription. The photographed signed original remains the controlling source. No analytical findings are made on this page.

[SIMT250901-20250901-0012]
---

[SIMT250901-20250901-0014]
Dear Mr. Moloney,

[SIMT250901-20250901-0016]
I am writing to follow up on the Preliminary Assessment Report (PAR) conducted regarding the care you received from consultant Neurologist, Dr. Thomas Monaghan between July 2017 and March 2025. The PAR was initiated as part of our review process in response to your complaint that outlined concerns of bias and diagnostic anchoring that you feel affected your care. As a result, you allege Sinemet CR was not prescribed until March 2025, resulting in prolonged foot pain.

[SIMT250901-20250901-0018]
The PAR was presented to the Serious Incident Management Team (SIMT) on 27/8/2025. This meeting was chaired by Dr. Ramona McLoughlin, Clinical Director for Quality & Patient Safety HSE West and North West. Following presentation of the PAR, the multidisciplinary membership of the SIMT were satisfied that decisions made in relation to care at Galway University Hospital were appropriate. The SIMT were reassured by the clinical notes that evidence concern and careful consideration about starting Sinemet at such an early age, given the risk of associated side-effects. Alternative potential diagnoses were considered and Sinemet was first prescribed in 2021, therefore it was determined that there was no deficit in the care provided.

[SIMT250901-20250901-0020]
Yours sincerely,

[SIMT250901-20250901-0022]
Ms. Aoife Nolan

[SIMT250901-20250901-0023]
Quality and Patient Safety Coordinator, Galway University Hospital

[SIMT250901-20250901-0025]
Professor Tim O'Brien

[SIMT250901-20250901-0026]
Clinical Director, Medicine MCAN

[SIMT250901-20250901-0027]
HSE West and North West

[SIMT250901-20250901-0029]
---

[SIMT250901-20250901-0031]
**Redaction / source-status note:** Home address and signatures are omitted from the transcript. This photographed signed letter is preserved separately from `simt-outcome-2025-09-03.md`, a later-dated two-page PDF carrying materially similar but not identical wording. Both should remain visible until their document/version relationship is established. Neither document discloses the complete PAR, source schedule or SIMT deliberative record.

[SIMT250903-20250903-0012]
Dear Mr. Moloney,

[SIMT250903-20250903-0014]
I am writing to follow up on the Preliminary Assessment Report (PAR) conducted regarding the care you received from consultant Neurologist, Dr. Thomas Monaghan between July 2017 and March 2025. The PAR was initiated as part of Galway University Hospitals response to your complaint that outlined your concerns with decisions anchoring on foot pain not reflective of your case. [SIMT250903-20250903-0020]
Ms. Aoife Nolan

## ART16CL250906 — Article 16 clinical decision

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0001]
# 6 September 2025 — Article 16 decision concerning clinical records

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0003]
**Source type:** primary institutional/data-governance decision

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0004]
**Author/signatory:** Nuala Madden, SARS Co-ordinator, University Hospital Galway

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0005]
**Recipient:** Liam Moloney

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0006]
**Date:** 6 September 2025

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0007]
**Provenance:** transcribed from the primary PDF `Sept 6th Refusal.pdf` preserved in Gmail and directly read in the project.

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0008]
**Status:** faithful redacted transcription of the substantive decision. The original PDF remains the controlling source. No analytical findings are made on this page.

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0010]
---

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0012]
Dear Mr Moloney,

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0014]
I refer to your request made on 12th June, 2025 for the erasure of a record, held in your medical file at Galway University Hospital. Firstly, I wish to apologise for the delay in issuing you a decision.

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0016]
Your request in summary refers to the following:

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0018]
1. “Rectify all relevant documents where the absence of my core symptoms (severe, disabling foot pain) misrepresents my diagnostic journey.”

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0019]
2. “Annotate all files (referrals, discharge summaries, diagnostic letters) that present a conclusive Parkinson’s diagnosis, noting the lack of dopaminergic diagnostic trial and subsequent evidence in 2025.”

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0021]
The decision has been made that the personal information contained in your medical records to which your application refers is not judged to be misleading for the following reasons.

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0023]
The HSE relies on Articles 6 and 9 of the GDPR as the legal basis for processing a patient’s personal and medical data. Article 6(1)(e) of GDPR is relied upon for processing the individual’s personal data which states that processing shall be lawful if “processing is necessary for the performance of a task carried out in the public interest or in the exercise of official authority vested in the controller”. For the HSE this lawful authority is vested in us through the Health Act 2004.

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0025]
Sections 36(1) & 52(1) of the Act cover suitable and specific measures of processing and of special categories of personal data for the provision of healthcare. I am satisfied that the HSE has met its legal requirements under the Act and is in compliance with GDPR.

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0027]
A diagnosis in a medical file is the reflection of the professional opinion of the doctor at that moment of time and based on the information available to that moment in time. On this basis the HSE has a lawful basis for continued processing of your medical records in accordance with Article 7(1) of GDPR.

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0029]
As a consequence, it remains factually accurate to record the opinion or diagnosis that was made and what the opinion or diagnosis was, such data would not be “inaccurate” as it is a factually accurate reflection that an opinion or diagnosis occurred and what the opinion or diagnosis was at that point in time and is still necessary in relation to the purposes for which the personal data was originally collected or otherwise processed and is consistent with Article 17(3)(c) in providing comprehensive medical assessment and treatment of the individual.

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0031]
In addition, a medical opinion or diagnosis provides important context for any subsequent medical treatment decisions made by Health Care providers. A prior opinion or diagnosis may explain why certain medications were prescribed or certain treatment plans were undertaken. Maintaining the medical record in its current form, serves the public interest by ensuring the service user and the treating Healthcare Professionals have a reliable record of what has taken place.

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0033]
Following consideration of the provisions of the above legislation, it has been decided to refuse your request, as there is an obligation on the HSE and its staff to keep accurate records which are essential for the continuity of care and comprehensive enough to allow Health Practitioners to be fully informed of factors which impact on the provision of care to be provided.

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0035]
Rights to appeal

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0037]
If you are unhappy with this decision you may make a complaint in writing to the Office of the Data Protection Commission.

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0039]
Yours sincerely,

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0041]
Nuala Madden

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0042]
SARS Co-ordinator,

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0043]
University Hospital Galway.

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0045]
---

[ART16CL250906-20250906-0047]
**Redaction / scope note:** postal addresses, signature image and administrative footer are omitted. This is the clinical-record Article 16 decision. It is distinct from the separate 6 September 2025 QPS closure-letter decision.

## ART16QPS250906 — Article 16 QPS decision

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0001]
# 6 September 2025 - Article 16 decision concerning QPS closure letters

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0003]
**Source type:** primary administrative / data-rights decision

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0004]
**Author/signatory:** Nuala Madden, SARS Coordinator, Galway University Hospitals

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0005]
**Recipient:** Liam Moloney

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0006]
**Date:** 6 September 2025

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0007]
**Underlying request:** Article 16 request dated 15 June 2025 concerning QPS closure letters

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0008]
**Provenance:** transcribed from the original two-page PDF preserved as `Sept 6th refusal II.pdf`, located in the connected Gmail account and directly read.

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0009]
**Status:** faithful redacted transcription. The original PDF remains the controlling source. No analytical findings are made on this page.

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0011]
---

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0013]
Dear Mr. Moloney,

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0015]
I refer to your request dated 15th June, 2025 made under Article 16 of the General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR) for the:

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0017]
> “Immediate rectification of all Quality and Patient Safety (QPS) ‘closure letters’ issued by Galway University Hospital that

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0018]
>

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0019]
> - Invoke the Health Act 2004 as justification for rerouting complaints back to the original clinician; and

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0020]
> - Use the term ‘assessment’ to describe an undocumented non-transparent internal process that classifies complaints as ‘solely clinical’ without independent review or appeal.”

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0022]
I wish to advise that the GDPR does provide individuals with the right to rectification of inaccurate personal data and the right to erasure in specific circumstances. However, your request does not seem to relate to the rectification of your personal data.

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0024]
Personal data as defined under the GDPR means any information relating to an identified or identifiable natural person (‘data subject’); therefore a Quality and Patient Safety “closure letters” issued by Galway University Hospital does not relate to you as a data subject.

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0026]
Therefore, my decision is to refuse this request for the amendment of records, as it does not relate to your personal data and does not come within the scope of personal data as defined under the GDPR.

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0028]
If you are unhappy with this decision, you may make a complaint in writing to the Office of the Data Protection Commissioner.

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0030]
Yours sincerely,

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0032]
Nuala Madden

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0033]
SARS Coordinator

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0034]
Galway University Hospitals

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0036]
---

[ART16QPS250906-20250906-0038]
**Redaction / source-status note:** Home address, regulator postal address and signature image are omitted. This source establishes the controller's decision and reasoning as communicated on 6 September 2025. It does not, by itself, determine whether that GDPR interpretation was legally correct.

## ART16260114 — Renewed Article 16 and 18 request

[ART16260114-20260114-0001]
# 14 January 2026 — Renewed Article 16 / Article 18 request

[ART16260114-20260114-0003]
**Source type:** primary data-subject correspondence

[ART16260114-20260114-0004]
**Author:** Liam Moloney

[ART16260114-20260114-0005]
**Recipient:** GUH Data Protection Officer

[ART16260114-20260114-0006]
**Date:** 14 January 2026

[ART16260114-20260114-0007]
**Reference:** C-25-7-2074

[ART16260114-20260114-0008]
**Provenance:** transcribed from the primary Gmail correspondence preserved in the connected mailbox.

[ART16260114-20260114-0009]
**Status:** faithful redacted transcription of the substantive request. The original email remains controlling. No analytical findings are made on this page.

[ART16260114-20260114-0011]
---

[ART16260114-20260114-0013]
Following DPC correspondence, Liam re-engaged with GUH and expressly framed the message as a renewed Article 16 request read with Articles 5(1)(d), 12, 18, 19 and 24 GDPR. He asserted that the Article 18 restriction invoked on 4 August 2025 remained in force and asked the controller to reconcile disputed data through rectification, annotation, qualification or lawful refusal.

[ART16260114-20260114-0015]
The request relied on a later dystonia diagnosis and argued that this changed the accuracy/completeness position concerning historic records and downstream governance use. It also alleged that SIMT had relied on disputed data while restriction was asserted.

[ART16260114-20260114-0017]
The requested reconciliation included the continued representation of Parkinson’s disease, omitted presenting symptoms/dystonia-consistent gait features, later pharmacological evidence, SIMT reliance on disputed data, and the characterisation of earlier rectification requests.

[ART16260114-20260114-0019]
---

[ART16260114-20260114-0021]
**Source-boundary note:** This source proves the renewed request, the allegations advanced and the continued Article 18 position asserted by the data subject. It does not prove that the later diagnosis had the legal effect claimed, that restriction had been accepted/implemented by the controller, or that SIMT processing breached GDPR.

## QPS260126 — QPS governance response

[QPS260126-20260126-0001]
# 26 January 2026 — QPS Governance Response

[QPS260126-20260126-0003]
**Source type:** primary later institutional / governance email

[QPS260126-20260126-0004]
**Author:** Emily Comerford, on behalf of the Quality & Patient Safety Team

[QPS260126-20260126-0005]
**Recipient:** Liam Moloney

[QPS260126-20260126-0006]
**Date:** 26 January 2026

[QPS260126-20260126-0007]
**Subject:** `RE: Subject: Governance Re-engagement: April 2025 Procedural Configuration and Continuing Reliance (as of 15 January 2026)`

[QPS260126-20260126-0008]
**Provenance:** read directly from the connected Gmail account. This page is a transcription of the institutional response body. No analytical findings are made on this page.

[QPS260126-20260126-0010]
---

[QPS260126-20260126-0012]
Dear Mr. Moloney,

[QPS260126-20260126-0014]
Thank you for your correspondence.

[QPS260126-20260126-0016]
The Quality & Patient Safety Department has considered the governance issues raised. The complaint assessment and subsequent SIMT review were undertaken using the information, clinical presentations, and investigations available at the time, and the determinations reached were appropriate, specific to a predefined timeframe, and compliant with established governance frameworks. A subsequent diagnosis of dystonia in December 2025 does not undermine the validity of those processes or outcomes. The SIMT assesses the quality and appropriateness of care based on contemporaneous data.

[QPS260126-20260126-0018]
In that regard, the updated diagnosis does not necessitate reopening governance reviews, altering prior determinations, or retrospectively amending historical medical records. Updated diagnoses supersede earlier working diagnoses clinically, but do not render prior governance processes unlawful or unsafe. On this basis, QPS does not identify a current governance risk arising from continued reliance on the completed processes, nor is escalation to another governance function indicated.

[QPS260126-20260126-0020]
Thank you for bringing these matters to our attention and for the opportunity to respond.

[QPS260126-20260126-0022]
Kind regards,

[QPS260126-20260126-0024]
Emily Comerford

[QPS260126-20260126-0025]
On Behalf of the Quality and Patient Safety Team

[QPS260126-20260126-0027]
---

[QPS260126-20260126-0029]
**Source-status note:** This source establishes what QPS stated and decided in January 2026. It is a new governance act, but it does not by itself add an independent historical clinical evidential origin for the 2017 foot/podiatry proposition. The original Gmail message remains the controlling source.

[PRKN26-20260204-0013]
Dear Dr Brennan,

[PRKN26-20260204-0015]
I received the results of Liam Moloney's genetic tests. They confirm that he has two pathogenic variants in the Parkin gene as predicted. His Parkinsonism and dystonia are due to compound heterozygous mutations in the Parkin gene. I plan to see him again in clinic.

[PRKN26-20260204-0017]
Yours sincerely,

[PRKN26-20260204-0019]
Dr Sean O'Riordan

[PRKN26-20260204-0020]
Consultant Neurologist

[PRKN26-20260204-0022]

[DS17-GEN-0932]
Parkinsons? —- Typical / Atypical?

[DS17-GEN-0934]
We think that the cause is a Parkinson's disease. However in many respects it is of

[DS17-GEN-0935]
course an atypical presentation:

[DS17-GEN-0937]
###lack of prominent tremor.

[DS17-GEN-0939]
###213 years already i.e. very young age of onset

[DS17-GEN-0941]
### symmetry is unusual - while he has asymmetry - to a degree, his movement

[DS17-GEN-0942]
disorder is more prominently symmetrical than assymetrical. This can be a feature of

[DS17-GEN-0943]
some genetic Parkinsons

[DS17-GEN-0945]
Form of Parkinsons/Parkinsonism?

[DS17-GEN-0947]
In essence, most Parkinson's disease disorders are disorders of a protein called

[DS17-GEN-0948]
Synuclein or how Synuclein is handled - - whereas the Parkinson's plus disorders can

[DS17-GEN-0949]
be disorders of protein Synculein, or range of other protein disorders e.g. very

[DS17-GEN-0950]
prominently protein Tau.

[DS17-GEN-0953]
It is an unpleasant analogy - and of course this is a very sensitive and deeply personal

[DS17-GEN-0954]
subject--- however an effective practical analogy which I have heard patients come up

[DS17-GEN-0955]
with themselves Is perhaps the nature of: "rust" and rust “spreading”.

[DS17-GEN-0957]
Any individual person can conceptulise that rust may be of different forms. While most

[DS17-GEN-0958]
people think of iron oxide as “rust” - - we all know that other forms of rust ---

[DS17-GEN-0959]
depending on the underlying metal --- can develop,

[DS17-GEN-0961]
In a similar way, most Parkinson's disease protein abnormalities are of synculein

[DS17-GEN-0962]
whereas there are other forms of protein abnormalities in some parkinsonian disorders.

[DS17-GEN-0963]
Additionally in other conditions there are ways Indeed that the same protein can

[DS17-GEN-0964]
present in different individuals - producing different disease forms (e.g. synuclein

[DS17-GEN-0965]
disorder also causes MSA and Lewy body disease - quite separately to more ordinary

[DS17-GEN-0966]
Parkinson’s disease).

[DS17-GEN-0968]
Obviously there Is a question of whether the previously used recreational drugs may

[DS17-GEN-0969]
have been a factor.

[DS17-GEN-0971]
I suppose it is plausible. It is possible that recreational drugs used that may have been

[DS17-GEN-0972]
quite harmless in the rest of the population but may have accelerated the situation in

[DS17-GEN-0973]
[Patient] --- however I wouldn’t have any great confidence in that explanation.

[DS17-GEN-0975]
The larger question is whether the formulation and chemica! composition of any of the

[DS17-GEN-0976]
agents previously used could have caused an MPTP like effect causing his

[DS17-GEN-0977]
Parkinsonsism. Again I am reluctant to accept that as an explanation either.

[DS17-GEN-0979]
I suspect that this was always going to happen to [Patient] and J don’t want him having

[DS17-GEN-0980]
any idea that this was “caused”.

[DS17-GEN-0982]
Himself [Patient] was worried that the alcohol was a factor? Aside from this being

[DS17-GEN-0996]
Genetics

[DS17-GEN-0998]
I have explained to him the difficulties with the genetic assessment.

[DS17-GEN-1000]
One of the chief difficulties with this is the question of whether a firm genetic diagnosis

[DS17-GEN-1001]
will actually actively change management?

[DS17-GEN-1003]
This question is (in the majority of cases) usually answered in the negative.

[DS17-GEN-1005]
In a way to explain genetics, I have in a very simplistic manner broken it up Into 3

[DS17-GEN-1006]
different concepts:

[DS17-GEN-1008]
-#1 what I term for [Patient]‘s understanding as “strong genetics”

[DS17-GEN-1010]
---where genetic disorders run very strongly through a family. We would terms these

[DS17-GEN-1011]
usually Autosomal Dominant.This is clearly not the case in [Patient]*s case and therefore it

[DS17-GEN-1012]
is not likely that these apply. It would not apply to most individuals with Parkinson's

[DS17-GEN-1013]
either. It is not a worry as he has no children as yet.

[DS17-GEN-1015]
#2 what I term for for [Patient]’s understanding as "moderate genetics"

[DS17-GEN-1017]
---I use this example to explain where a person may have a range of different genes,

[DS17-GEN-1018]
many of them are currently unknown but which confer “susceptibilities” to Parkinson's.

[DS17-GEN-1019]
Depending on the combination of susceptibilities a person has genetically, then these,

[DS17-GEN-1020]
and various components of environment can contribute to the development of a

[DS17-GEN-1021]
Parkinson’ s disorder. This is a theory that probably applies to most people who actually

[DS17-GEN-1022]
develop Parkinson's disease “sporadically” or “idiopathically” who may have

[DS17-GEN-1023]
susceptibility genes that we do not understand the role of as yet or known genes with

[DS17-GEN-1024]
variations (e.g. In the LRRK2/ SNCA / GBA genes), as well as a range of possible

[DS17-GEN-1025]
environmental factors.

[DS17-GEN-1027]
#3---what I term for [Patient]’s understandinf as "weak genetics"

[DS17-GEN-1029]
---by this we mean the classical autosomal recessively inherited disorders that can

[DS17-GEN-1030]
occur within a family but which would be very unlikely to affect other family members.

[DS17-GEN-1031]
It is quite plausible that [Patient] may have one of these disorders as the cause for his

[DS17-GEN-1032]
Parkinsons/Parkinsonisms.

[DS17-GEN-1034]
More specifically given a number of issues including his very young age of onset; the

[DS17-GEN-1035]
akinetic rigidity; the lack of a Dominant family history; and the marked symmetry; as

[DS17-GEN-1036]
well as how cognitively excellent [Patient] is, there is a question of whether the indicators

[DS17-GEN-1037]
are that he does have a PRKN gene disorder (PARK 2)?

[DS17-GEN-1040]
Nervethless aspects may point to rarer monogenic explanations such as PARK 6 or

[DS17-GEN-1041]
PARK7 or even PARK 9 (the neuropathy question).

[DS17-GEN-1043]
A summary of where we are with Genetics can be seen from this summary from the

[DS17-GEN-1044]
National Institutes of health in the US:

[DS17-GEN-1046]
Genetic Testing in Parkinson*s Disease

[DS17-GEN-1048]
Genetic testing has recently become available for the parkin and PINK1 genes. Parkin is

[DS17-GEN-1049]
a large gene and testing is difficult. At the current stage of understanding, testing Is

[DS17-GEN-1050]
likely to give a meaningful result only for people who develop the condition before the

[DS17-GEN-1051]
age of 30 years. PINK1 appears to be a rare cause of inherited Parkinson‘s disease. A

[DS17-GEN-1052]
small percentage (~2 percent) of those developing the condition at an early age appear

[DS17-GEN-1053]
to carry mutations in the PINK1 gene. Genetic testing for the PARK7, SNCA and LRRK2

[DS17-GEN-1054]
genes is also available.

[DS17-GEN-1056]
Individuals and families who are interested in having genetic testing can learn more

[DS17-GEN-1057]
about their risk for Parkinson's disease and the availability and accuracy of genetic

[DS17-GEN-1058]
testing for this disease by setting up an appointment with a genetics health

[DS17-GEN-1059]
professional. Genetic professionals work as members of health care teams providing

[DS17-GEN-1060]
information and support to individuals or families who have genetic disorders or may be

[DS17-GEN-1061]
at risk for inherited conditions. Genetic professionals can discuss the risks, benefits and

[DS17-GEN-1062]
limitations of available genetic testing for Parkinson‘s disease. See: Frequently Asked

[DS17-GEN-1063]
Questions about Genetic Counseling

[DS17-GEN-1065]
Another article on the genetics of Parkinsons written a couple of years ago from the

[DS17-GEN-1066]
Department here with [Senior Consultant] is enclosed within.

[DS17-GEN-1068]
Accordingly the only rea! current value of a "genetic" diagnosis is in terms of what it

[DS17-GEN-1069]
might tell us about the actua! neuropathological form of which neurodegenerative
